#4768. 6 Best Linen Sheets of 2026, Tested and Approved by AD Editors

There’s no doubt that linen sheets are a great option for a summer bedscape—they feel breathable and evoke the easygoing vibe of the season because of their relaxed look and feel. But when chilly weather hits, we think there’s no better time to round up this bedding. The fabric has become increasingly popular among our Shop AD editors in seasons like fall and winter. It’s great for layering without overheating, and it can create an effortlessly lived-in look in your bedroom thanks to its natural, slubby texture.

Linen truly has great range—it appears in Scandi-style beach houses and maximal, colorful city apartments. As of late, we’re seeing it offered in on-trend color palettes and patterns, like cottagecore-inspired botanical green gingham from Piglet in Bed. If you’re more inclined to choose a solid color, you can venture away from a plain white sheet set with soothing muted tones or vibrant colors. Another plus is that these sheets get softer from the first wash on (yes, it’s really true). Here are favorites to help you sleep peacefully through the seasons.

Evokes a countryside-inspired aesthetic, durable material, customizable bundle, Oeko-Tex certified

UK-based Piglet in Bed’s linen and linen-blend collections are a dream in the bedding category. Here’s the rundown: The brand makes the highest-quality pure linen and linen blend (55% European flax and 45% long-staple cotton) our editors have ever tried and has a huge color and pattern range to make your bed the true centerpiece in your room.

Contributor Yelena Moroz Alpert calls the brand’s sheets a cottagecore match made in heaven because of the ginghams, florals, and stripes in the mix. She has both the linen and linen-blend, the latter of which is pictured above in sage green. “I suppose these linen-blend [sheets] feel a bit more brushed while the linen feels slightly more textured,” Alpert says. “Think of the linen-blend as cotton sheets that have seen the best of times, they feel lived-in, like you’re sleeping in a nest.”

In terms of the pure linen, Alpert chose botanical green gingham because it goes with everything she tries. “I’ve paired it with a set of desert sand-colored linen sheets to evoke an English cottage setup, but it will also go beautifully with my gold crinkled velvet duvet cover and shams if I want to veer into a more maximalist look,” she says. “I think it will be a natural fit for when I pull out my chocolate-hued quilt.” Even after countless washes in the last year, Alpert says these sheets feel just as sturdy (no frayed threads or pilling in sight) and haven’t changed in color one bit, which is pretty rare when it comes to linen bed sheets.

The cost is up there, but contributor Zoë Sessums makes the case for investing in the 100% linen too: “These are truly some substantial sheets, so the weight and sturdy feeling were noticeable first. The more I sleep in these sheets, the softer they get—it’s true. I also find the linen to be an ideal match for my typically hot body temperature. You really get what you pay for, so I would say these are A+++ sheets.”

Color variety, designs available for nearly any bedroom style, approachable price point

The midweight might feel heavy for hot sleepers, has a slightly rougher texture

After seeing 35 neutral and vibrant shades and prints (like trendy butter yellow colors and gingham and striped patterns) up for grabs, senior shopping editor Nashia Baker knew she wanted to try this Quince linen set to style her bed. Quince’s garment-washed, midweight, 100% European flax sheets are one of the more affordable options in this list at under $200 for a queen-size set (and who doesn’t sleep better knowing they got a great deal?). “Even though my bedroom style can vary, I’ve been inspired by a Mediterranean-meets-minimalist aesthetic these days—which is exactly why Quince’s linen bedding has been a mainstay on my mattress,” Baker says. “I love its airy nature along with the gorgeous Bordeaux color on my bed that complements the rest of my rich-toned decor.” Baker loves layering this set with her cashmere duvet cover in fall and winter for a cozy and breathable bedscape. Though she warns these aren’t as soft as some of the heirloom-quality linens in this list, she still thinks they’re worth it for the price and overall look.

Made with sustainably sourced French linen, gets softer with each wash and hold up well over time, pinstripe adds an unexpected texture to your bedscape

Short break-in period required to reach peak softness

Linen sheets aren’t usually known for being soft, but these are an exception. What makes Sijo’s set different is its stonewashed treatment, which allows it to soften well over time, as opposed to basic sets that feel scratchy no matter how much they wear. Contributor Blake Bakkila adds that the patterning gives these sheets more of a slubby feel. “I’m a sucker for stripes, and the pinstripe colorway isn’t a classic printed-on design,” she says. “Rather, there’s additional thread that creates the pinstripe pattern. It’s a subtle touch that can go a long way, especially in a room lacking texture.”

In the last few months with the bedding, Bakkila remains impressed by the durability—she hasn’t seen any wear and tear, even with several consecutive washes. They also feel incredibly breathable. “In my second trimester of pregnancy, sleeping well is a rare luxury,” she says. “But with these sheets, I feel both comforted and cool all night long—no easy feat when you’re pregnant and in the midst of a heat wave [in California].”

Deep pocket designs, European sizes available, several bold colorways

Linoto takes linen very seriously. The company offers items across the decor spectrum, but the sheets are the real stars—they’re handmade in upstate New York with fine Italian and Belgian linen. Dozens of versatile, deeply saturated hues like deep red Barolo wine and beeswax make this set ideal for a well-curated, maximalist bedroom. (There are neutrals like natural oatmeal if you’re more of a minimalist, though.) Prewashed with nontoxic detergent, they arrive ready to be loved. Contributor Erika Owen is a fan of their good looks and durability. “I also love the lived-in look they constantly have, thanks to the easy wrinkling,” she adds. Since the GSM reaches up to 210, these are considered more of a heavyweight option for the winter or cold sleepers year-round. Need deep pockets or European sizes? They got you covered there. If you’re looking for the best organic linen sheets, Linoto has an option for that too.

Crisp and structured feel, breathable cotton-linen fabric blend, muted flower pattern works acts as a neutral print

Floral sheets can get a bad rap for looking, well, flowery, but Lulu and Georgia’s sheets feel like a departure from stereotypical styles. The muted tones blend easily in an earthy fall and winter color palette, and the bedding can layer easily in rooms with a neutral design style in general. The wildflowers add a touch of elegance, too, according to Alpert. “The print has this white outline that gives the bedding a 3D quality, especially when you look at it from further away,” she says. “It’s like the florals stand out and look like they have actually been block printed or that the bedding is made of jacquard fabric, giving it a very much elevated look.”

In terms of texture, the material has what Alpert calls a “percale-meets-linen” feel. “The cotton-linen blend from Lulu and Georgia is certainly more on the crisper side, as opposed to the worn-in texture of bedding that is made of 100% linen,” she says. While Alpert says the texture took some getting used to, she reaches for the set pretty often now because of its refined design. “Since the weight is a bit heavier, the bedding looks more structured and polished when it’s all made up,” she adds. “It also has a cooling quality, which I especially appreciated when I was sick and had a light fever.”

Features stunning, traditional Palestinian-inspired motifs, moisture-wicking material, heirloom-quality softness

“The design is the biggest performer here—these sheets look and feel expensive,” says shopping writer Julia Harrison. “The careful embroidery is like nothing else on the market, and I’m browsing the market all day, every day.” This 100% linen bedding is designed by artisans in Jaipur and takes inspiration from traditional Palestinian Tree of Life and Mountain motifs.

Harrison tells us that she’s more of a percale person, and these sheets don’t feel as cooling as she’s used to when climbing in bed, but they still have a moisture-wicking and breathable quality from the purely natural fabric. Above all that, the texture is truly top-tier. “It’s incredibly soft and it’s totally unperturbed by many turns in the wash (and I’m using NYC laundromats, which are notoriously tough on fabrics),” she says. Harrison’s final thought is that the price feels right based on the quality and detail of the bedding. “That feels rare to me; things often feel severely overpriced, and what’s affordable often cuts corners in terms of material blends or manufacturing processes,” she says. “This is a true, honest, fantastic product.”

There is one question we always get about testing: Do AD editors actually sleep on all the sheets featured in our roundups? The answer is yes. Instead of lab testing or giving sheets a quick once-over in a store, our editors brought these linen sheets in their homes to style them, launder them, and sleep on them (just like our readers would), and look for qualities like a relaxed look and feel. These real-world-tested sets are selected based on a variety of price points and designs to fit any shopper’s needs. Linen fibers, which are derived from the flax plant, are naturally hypoallergenic, absorbent, and antibacterial, so all of us also looked for sheets without synthetic fibers to suit sleepers with sensitive skin.

Some other favorites that have made this list before are the Parachute Linen Sheet Set, made in Portugal, because of the organic colorways available for a minimalist or bohemian-inspired bedroom, and the Citizenry Stonewashed Linen Sheet Set in soothing hues like seaglass for a coastal space. Those were taken out of the story because of the vast colorways and prints available in the other picks in this list, but AD editors still stand behind the quality. Pom Pom at Home’s set is a premium option Baker loves because of its supple texture, which is extremely rare for linen, but it’s a pricier pick.

We also took the Parachute Linen Tencel Sheet Set out of this guide after Baker poked a hole through her set simply after taking the sheets off the bed to put them in the washing machine, so we recommend going with the brand’s sturdy, 100% linen instead of this blend.

Linen sheets naturally soften after the first couple of washes. Always mind the care instructions, but typically a machine-wash and tumble-dry allows for streamlined upkeep that can help relax the fibers.

Yes! Linen sheets of the highest quality are sustainably made with flax fibers that are airy and breathable by nature. Depending on your texture preferences, the lived-in feel of linen could be best for summer months or year-round use. These organic fibers naturally wick away moisture, and AD editors say linen feels significantly more textured than other cooling fabrics derived from bamboo or eucalyptus. Sure, there’s a breeziness to linen, but our editors note bamboo and eucalyptus sheets are often silky and cool to the touch.

All of the sets in this list are good quality, but we think sheets made of 100% European flax (which typically includes fibers grown in France and Belgium) are the highest of the high. It’s super soft from the long-staple materials in the fabric. From our experience, these fibers are durable and hold color well, so we don’t notice much pilling, if any at all, from wash to wash.

Shopping for linen sheets means you’ll come across GSM in product descriptions. This stands for grams per square meter and measures the weight of the sheets. Our list has around 150- to 200-plus GSM measurements. The 150 to 200 range is usually best for an all-season set since it’s more of a medium weight. Sheets above a 200 GSM are considered heavyweight, which is nice if you live somewhere with sub-zero temperatures every winter. But even if you don’t, the fabric has a substantial, soft feel for people just looking for a cozy and comforting bedding set. Anything below 150 or 160 or so is better if you’re a hot sleeper.

What Are the Benefits of Using Linen Sheets Over Cotton?

Linen and cotton, especially percale weaves, are two of the best types of fabrics for hot sleepers since they’re so breathable. Slubbed and naturally uneven, linen carries a more relaxed, coastal-inspired vibe. Cotton percale, however, is more structured, polished, and reminiscent of bedscapes at high-end hotels.

“Percale is woven with a one-over-one-under weave for a matte look that is cool to the touch, and offers a crisp, smooth feeling, like a freshly-pressed button-down shirt,” Lainey Hollis, senior vice president of product creation at Boll & Branch, previously told AD.

And if you can’t decide, go for both. Our top pick overall is Piglet in Bed’s linen-blend fabric, which features European linen and long-staple cotton.

How Do I Choose the Best Linen Sheets for My Bed?

You could spend decades testing out the best linen sheets, or you can take our word for it. During our reviews, we always consider the following key factors:

Material: There are 100% linens and linen blends, and we recommend both types, but you also want to review the origins and quality of those fibers. As we mentioned above, 100% European flax is preferred. If you’re seeking out the best of the best, family-owned bedding brand Libeco Home is one of six weavers in the world with Belgian Linen Label. This guarantees their linen is woven in Belgium and made with European flax fibers.

Prewashing treatment: Prewashed linens typically have a more relaxed look and feel, which makes them feel softer and worn-in from the moment they’re dropped at your doorstep. For example, the Linoto sheets we highlighted in this guide are prewashed with nontoxic detergent and ready for use upon arrival. For all future washes, Mary Gagliardi, Clorox’s in-house scientist and cleaning expert, previously shared, “care instructions typically recommend the delicate cycle, with lower agitation and spin speeds that help reduce wrinkling—which is good but also means washing with cold water, which is less effective at cleaning.”

Weight: Your sheet’s weight will also determine the overall feel. Linen sheets are commonly used in the warmer months, though options ranging from 150 to 200 GSM should work well year-round (especially when you layer with a duvet cover, quilt, or blanket when temps drop).

#4767. Valve roll out Steam Achievement Sets in beta, splitting up games’ perpetually growing trophy masses into DLC and season buckets

No more having DLC decimate your precious 100% completion badge

Steam has a new feature in beta aimed at helping completionists feel like they've got a bit more of a handle on their never-ending quests to 100% games whose trophy lists keep on growing and evolving. Steam achievements can now be grouped into sets determined by the developers, creating a means of separating off the likes of DLC trophies from the base game's to-do list, thereby making it clearer what you've done so far and where you might want to focus next.

"Games today live longer than ever, continuing to receive updates through new DLC, expansions, and seasons," Valve put it in their announcement post. "But achievements added with new game content can be hard to discover and can accidentally break the completion status of players who have already finished the game. We have designed Achievement Sets to help address these types of issues."

As such, developers can use these sets to group their game's achievements into specific collections within the overall total, each of which has its own progress tracking bar. For example, they might choose to split off single player achievements from multiplayer ones, making it easier to tell if you've mastered solo play or gotten all of your plaudits with mates. Perhaps they'll prefer to spilt it so there's one set for the base game, then individual sets for each DLC added or class on offer.

Games with rolling seasons can also work in archivable temporary sets that will only show up as earnable when that particular bit of content or holiday is running, thereby eliminating any risk of you having one hidden trophy left to go that's actually no longer grabbable because it's based on a bit of the game that's long gone.

Out of the gate, the list of games that support these achievement sets is as follows:

As part of this update, Valve are also beta testing an overhauled achievements page. "The new page gives you a summary of your overall progress, progress in each achievement set, and details on individual achievements," they wrote. "Global Stats, or the per-achievement completion rates, are now available as a toggle here rather than on a separate page. Search has also been expanded with filters for specific achievement sets and for locked or unlocked achievements. Plus, there's a little extra joy when you complete all your achievement sets." You can also compare your totals with up to three mates.

To give the new sets a whirl or chuck out the overhauled page, you'll need to opt into the Steam Client Beta. Instructions on how to do so can be found here.

#4766. Rattlesnake bite venom is based on their personality

Published Oct 22, 2026 1:42 PM EDT

Rattlesnake venom is a complex blend of necrotic toxins designed to quickly disrupt blood coagulation and destroy tissue cells. This effective biological weapon comes at a major premium, however. Snakes must regularly replenish their venom reserves, which means they need to dole out just the right amount with each bite. Injecting too little venom into prey may allow their next meal to escape, while delivering too much can leave a rattlesnake defenseless against its own predators.

But that doesn’t mean rattlesnakes adhere to a one-bite-fits-all policy. According to researchers at California’s Loma Linda University, the amount of venom routinely varies by individual reptile. Their recent findings published in Frontiers in Ethology indicate these dosage ranges aren’t randomly distributed, and that snakes and their venom are much more complicated than many people realize.

“This study gives us a better understanding that reptiles, such as rattlesnakes, are behaviorally complex and have personalities like humans do,” explained Loma Linda University biologist and study co-author Chelsea Martin.

Martin and her colleague, William Hayes, focused on the preferences of the Pacific rattlesnake (Crotalus oreganus helleri). On three occasions over a four-week window, the team offered 48 snakes the chance to sink their fangs into a membrane-covered beaker. Previous experiments mainly focused on the frequency of bites, but Martin and Hayes also measured the amount of injected venom in each instance. They then compared the data across snakes.

“The results were striking in their consistency,” the study’s authors wrote in an accompanying Phys.org rundown.

The team also reported measured behaviors like aggressiveness, bite willingness, and the amount of venom amount were statistically significant and repeatable across many of the snakes. Additionally, their study further debunks the lingering myth that larger snakes actually produce less venom.

“Larger snakes expended more venom than smaller snakes, as expected given their greater capacity for venom storage,” explained the biologists, while noting that actual delivered levels still occasionally varied regardless of size.

The study’s authors emphasize that their findings shouldn’t be held against the rattlesnakes themselves. While often feared, the snakes generally do everything they can to avoid confrontations with larger animals like humans. Bites are almost always the result of an unintentional encounter in the wild rather than an overly aggressive serpent. If anything, it’s people who aren’t paying attention to their surroundings, and not the other way around.

“One practical lesson remains unchanged: No rattlesnake should be approached or handled on the assumption that it will behave predictably,” the authors said. “Consistent individual differences do not make any particular snake safe.”

#4765. Is Nathan Fielder the Peak of Modern Masculinity?

You Can See Everything co-director Lance Oppenheim calls him a “very unconventionally hyper-masculine man.” We get what he’s talking about.

IF YOU’RE EVEN somewhat interested in movies, you’ve heard all about You Can See Everything (including from us at Men’s Health). In the week and change since the movie was officially released, the film—which combines unprecedented access to former Theranos CEO and convicted fraudster Elizabeth Holmes and her partner Billy Evans with a meta-narrative threaded by co-directors Nathan Fielder and Lance Oppenheim—has been discussed ad nauseam, sparking discourses about not only the facts realities of Holmes’s case and situation, but about the very nature of documentary films, language, and the truth.

And while the headlines of You Can See Everything are largely about the access to Holmes as a public figure and things she did or didn’t say, the reality is that it’s more of a Nathan Fielder movie than an Elizabeth Holmes movie. Fielder, who broke out with the docu-spoof series Nathan For You on Comedy Central before moving on to more surreal takes on similar subjects with HBO’s The Rehearsal and Paramount+ and Showtime’s The Curse, is at the center of the story all along, looking for something, anything, in Holmes and Evans as he talks to them. He’s searching less for the truth of the case—that’s already been investigated by the courts—and more for the truth of who Elizabeth is and why she’s behaved the way she has.

For a film of this structure to work, the person we’re along with for the ride needs to be someone we both understand and don’t mind spending that kind of time with. While You Can See Everything ultimately leaves us questioning everything we’ve just seen, Fielder, to his credit, is someone who makes the three-hour runtime an easy watch. He’s dry, yes—that’s his on-screen persona. But he’s also vulnerable and often humanistic. When he faces down Holmes, for example, in a scene where he shows her Abbott and Costello’s famous “Who’s On First?” routine for the first time, he reacts in a way most viewers could probably see themselves reacting.

Oppenheim did an interview with GQ, and he suggested that Fielder’s nature in the film makes him something of an emblem for modern men. “I feel like he's at his most masculine in You Can See Everything,” he said. “I'd always tell him, ‘You look so handsome in this.’ And I was just like, ‘I love being this close on your face. I'm so tight on your face right now and you look amazing.’ And he'd be like, ‘Dude, you could see my blackheads and my zits and stuff.’ I'd be like, ‘You look great. I actually think it makes you look better.’”

His quote alludes to the way Fielder carries himself in the movie: as something of an audience surrogate (at least during the first part of the film), and someone the audience can see themselves in. This marks a shift from Fielder’s previous on-screen personas, when he was a bit more of an on-screen joker or troll, egging his subjects a bit more into saying or doing something that will make them look silly. Here, he’s basically a blank slate for the audience to graft onto.

It’s interesting to read the quotes from Oppenheim, who last month also directed Primetime, a scripted A24 film starring Robert Pattinson. Pattinson, of course, is a traditionally handsome movie star, someone who’s played heartthrob roles like Edward Cullen in Twilight or literal superheroes like Bruce Wayne in The Batman. There’s, of course, a vision of masculinity in that. But when you look at someone like Fielder who is a normal guy—there’s even more of a mirror image being reflected to those of us watching in the theater or at home.

You Can See Everything understands the power in this unassuming parallel. Outside of Fielder, Evans is the only other male figure substantially present in the film, and he does partake in some more traditional expressions of masculine energy: He goes to the gym, he shoots guns in a range once he moves to Texas, and he even partakes in a shower scene of full-frontal male nudity as Fielder looks on. But it’s that parallel that Oppenheim implies is important—Fielder isn’t ever nervous or uncomfortable. “He's a very unconventionally hyper-masculine man,” Oppenheim said. “That's a compliment, I guess.”

The movie works because of the access we’re getting, and the way Fielder and Oppenheim set things up and draw us in and spit us out at the end. But it also works because Fielder so clearly knows who he is and understands who he is—blackheads be damned. And when the result is You Can See Everything, does anything else really matter?

#4764. PUBG's extraction shooter spin-off, Black Budget, is finally going to be playable next month

The most interesting development in the PUBG universe, PUBG: Black Budget, is almost ready to show itself off to a wider group of players.

You’d be forgiven for not remembering PUBG: Black Budget, the extraction off-shoot of the original battle royale shooter, PUBG. Black Budget has been sort of floating around the internet for months, but Krafton never really committed to revealing core details about the game, or when players will actually get to play it.

This changes today, as the publisher - along with the game’s developer, PUBG Studios, have finally lifted the lid off the upcoming shooter.

Black Budget is described as a “tactical” extraction shooter. Squads of players infiltrate a mysterious island trapped in a time loop. There’s a bit of a STALKER element here with anomalies and various, unpredictable environmental shifts.

Coli, the island in question, hides a research facility that holds secrets and artefacts, which is what players will be attempting to extract. This is very much a PvPvE shooter, though PUBG Studios says the focus will be on exploration and discovery, rather than constant combat.

The game’s first set of closed alpha tests is set for December. The first takes place December 12-14, and another is scheduled for December 19-21. That’s six days across two weeks, with the test only being available on PC. Both sessions will be open to players in North America, Europe, and Asia.

As it usually works with these sorts of tets on Steam, the game’s Steam page has a link where you can request access. If granted, you’ll be able to find the closed alpha in your library and download it when the time comes.

Though this is a closed test, Krafton confirmed that players will be able to capture and livestream their gameplay. In fact, watching content creators might be how you get access, as Black Budget is going to have a Twitch Drops event running alongside those sessions.

When I played Arc Raiders prior to its launch, I likened its tense and uncertain atmosphere to that of the early days of PUBG, so it’s going to be very interesting to see what actual PUBG does with the extraction shooter premise.

So far, it sounds like it’s going to be doing its own thing, leaning more into the adventure side of the subgenre, but we’ll have to see how it all shakes out when the tests start. Indeed, the BR-like constantly shrinking circle sounds like it could be a game-changing twist.

#4763. Oral GLP-1 Orforglipron (Foundayo) Achieves Weight Loss for Women of All Ages

  • A new analysis of previous studies found that orforglipron promoted weight loss in women regardless of their menopausal status.
  • This is important since the decline in estrogen after menopause can make weight loss more difficult.
  • Menopausal women also tend to gain weight around their midsection, which is associated with health risks.
  • The fact that the new GLP-1 comes in a pill form offers a convenient option for many women.
  • However, doctors stress that it is also important to select the treatment that is the most effective for you.

A new GLP-1 pill called orforglipron (Foundayo) has been shown to help women lose weight, regardless of menopausal status, according to new research.

A group of researchers, including senior author Hunter Thomas Hoffman, PharmD, a clinical research scientist affiliated with the drug’s manufacturer, Eli Lilly, analyzed data from two prior trials, ATTAIN-1 and ATTAIN-2, to arrive at this conclusion.

These studies reveal that this medication works equally well for women who have not yet gone through menopause, those who are currently experiencing it, and those who are postmenopausal. 

The findings were presented at the Annual Meeting of the European Association for the Study of Diabetes (EASD), held in Milan, Italy, from September 28 to October 2.

This is important because menopause often causes weight gain due to lower estrogen levels, making weight loss more difficult for many women. 

Orforglipron offers a promising new option for managing weight in women with obesity or overweight, especially those with type 2 diabetes.

In April of this year, Eli Lilly’s Foundayo received FDA approval as a weight-loss drug, making it the second GLP-1 pill for weight loss to enter the U.S. market after Novo Nordisk’s Wegovy.

Examining menopause and weight loss with orforglipron

The ATTAIN-1 and ATTAIN-2 studies involved women who were either obese or overweight and who had at least one medical condition related to their weight. 

Obesity was defined as a body mass index (BMI) of 30 or higher, and overweight as a BMI of 27 or higher. 

The participants were divided into groups based on their menopausal status: premenopausal (before menopause), perimenopausal (during the menopause transition), and postmenopausal (after menopause). 

Each group included between 142 and 225 females who either received orforglipron or placebo, an inactive pill with none of the drug in it.

The ATTAIN-1 study focused on people who were overweight or obese but did not have diabetes. ATTAIN-2 included participants who had both overweight or obesity and type 2 diabetes. 

The purpose of analyzing these two groups separately was to understand how orforglipron works in people with different health conditions.

The researchers measured several outcomes over a period of 72 weeks. They examined changes in body weight, waist circumference (the distance around the waist), and the waist-to-height ratio, a measure used to assess body fat distribution. 

They also tracked how many women reached specific weight loss goals, such as losing at least 5%, 10%, 15%, or 20% of their body weight, compared to those who took the inactive pill.

By studying these factors, the researchers were able to determine not only whether orforglipron helped women lose weight but also how much weight loss was achieved and how it affected their body shape.

Up to 14% weight loss with orforglipron regardless of menopause status

The results showed that women who took orforglipron experienced significant weight loss regardless of their menopausal status. 

In the ATTAIN-1 and ATTAIN-2 studies, premenopausal, perimenopausal, and postmenopausal women all lost an average of up to 14% of their body weight. 

This means that the medication was effective for women at different life stages, including those experiencing menopause, when weight gain is often a challenge.

In addition to losing body weight, women in the orforglipron groups also saw meaningful reductions in waist circumference. Waist measurements decreased by up to 12.5 centimeters (about 5 inches) across all menopausal groups in both studies. 

This reduction is important because greater visceral fat is associated with higher risks of heart disease and diabetes.

The waist-to-height ratio also improved for many women taking orforglipron. More participants treated with the medication moved into lower waist-to-height ratio categories than those who took the placebo. 

This indicates a healthier distribution of body fat and suggests a lower risk of heart disease.

When looking at the number of women who reached specific weight loss milestones, orforglipron performed much better than the placebo. Up to 83% of women on orforglipron lost at least 5% of their body weight, while 30% of women on placebo reached that same level of weight loss. 

Losing 5% or more of body weight is often considered clinically meaningful, as it can lead to improvements in blood pressure, blood sugar, and cholesterol levels.

Overall, these findings suggest that orforglipron is a promising new medication for weight management in women with obesity or overweight, including those who also have type 2 diabetes. 

This can be especially helpful for those navigating the challenges of menopause while trying to manage their weight.

Oral GLP-1 offers convenient weight loss option for women

James J. Chao, MD, FACS, Chief Medical Officer and co-founder of VedaNu Wellness, wasn’t involved in the research, but said the significance of these findings for postmenopausal women.

“The 14% is what’s grabbing headlines,” he told Healthline, “but up to 12.5 cm from the waistline is what I’m interested in.”

According to Chao, as females enter postmenopause, they tend to store more fat around their midsection.

“This is the type of fat that can cause heart disease,” he said. “So a pill that can help a 38-year-old woman lose the same amount as a 62-year-old breaks that myth.”

Additionally, in contrast to most GLP-1 drugs, which are injectable, it’s a once-a-day pill. You can also take it any time, Chao explained, making it more convenient.

“No needle, no refrigerator, and up to 83% of participants lost at least 5%,” he said. “Sometimes it’s the simple things that make a difference.”

Luca Moretti, MD, a medical doctor at Sapienza University of Rome, who wasn’t involved in the studies, added that convenience isn’t the only thing to consider when deciding whether to use orforglipron.

Obesity management should begin with an individualized plan that includes nutrition, physical activity, and the management of other risk factors.

“When these measures are not sufficient, adding pharmacological treatment can be considered together with a physician,” he told Healthline.

Moretti further advised speaking with your physician about any potential side effects, your existing medical conditions, other medications you are taking, and your treatment preferences.

“So the most useful question to ask a doctor is not simply, ‘Is Foundayo more convenient?’ but rather: ‘Among the available treatments, which one offers the best balance of effectiveness, tolerability, and practicality for me?'” he said.

#4762. Quantum fears already limiting Bitcoin’s growth, Stacks founder says

Speaking on Cointelegraph’s Chain Reaction podcast, Muneeb Ali warned that uncertainty over Bitcoin’s quantum security could deter institutional investors.

Concerns about quantum computing are slowing institutional investment in Bitcoin, according to Stacks founder Muneeb Ali, who warned that the network needs a credible roadmap for security upgrades to restore investor confidence.

“There is already a ceiling on the growth of Bitcoin because of quantum fears,” Ali said during an interview on Cointelegraph’s Chain Reaction podcast. He argued that uncertainty could lead risk-averse institutional investors to delay large Bitcoin purchases until developers establish a credible path toward quantum-resistant security.

Stacks is a Bitcoin layer-2 network designed to bring smart contracts and decentralized applications to Bitcoin. Ali co-founded the project, which uses Bitcoin as its underlying settlement layer.

Chain Reaction Podcast. Source: Cointelegraph

Ali warned that Bitcoin could enter a riskier period if developers fail to make meaningful progress toward quantum-resistant security by 2028. He said upgrades typically take years to implement, with additional time needed for users to move their holdings to new addresses.

Ali pointed to 2030 as a target for transitioning to quantum-resistant security, citing guidance from the US National Institute of Standards and Technology (NIST). He suggested advances in artificial intelligence could accelerate the development of quantum computers, potentially bringing that timeline forward to 2029.

Ali acknowledged that some Bitcoin proponents believe the quantum computing threat remains distant and concerns are overblown. He argued, however, that disagreements over the urgency of the threat could themselves undermine investor confidence, particularly without a credible upgrade roadmap.

Related: Justin Sun says Tron’s post-quantum cryptography has gone live on testnet

Despite his concerns, Ali said a sufficiently powerful quantum computer would more likely emerge from a major research laboratory than an unknown attacker. He suggested companies such as Google or Microsoft would have little incentive to use the technology to steal Bitcoin, given the potential legal consequences.

Ali also said a quantum security threat to Bitcoin would affect the broader cryptocurrency industry, even projects developing their own protections. “If Bitcoin gets hit, everyone gets hit downstream as well,” he said.

Meanwhile, developers are exploring ways to protect Bitcoin against quantum attacks. In September, StarkWare reported that researchers had reduced the estimated computing cost of preparing an experimental quantum-resistant Bitcoin transaction by 79%, following a weeklong optimization challenge.

The approach requires no changes to Bitcoin’s consensus rules, but the latest cost reductions have been demonstrated only in benchmark tests. StarkWare has described the technology as a last-resort measure and continues to advocate for a protocol-level upgrade as a longer-term solution.

#4761. Switching from GLP-1 Injections to Wegovy Pill Shows Additional Weight Loss of 4.1%

  • People with overweight or obesity who switched from an injectable GLP-1 weight loss medication to the Wegovy (semaglutide) pill lost an average of 4.1% of their body weight over 3 months.
  • After 3 months, about 2 in 5 participants had lost at least 5% of their body weight.
  • The proportion of participants with obesity decreased by 21.6 percentage points, from 87.1% at the start of the study to 65.5% after 3 months.

People with overweight or obesity who switch from an injectable GLP-1 medication to the Wegovy (semaglutide) pill may continue to lose weight, according to findings from the OCTANE study presented at the 2026 Meeting of the European Association for the Study of Diabetes (EASD) in Milan, Italy. 

The study is among the first real-world analyses to examine weight loss and treatment experiences among people who switched from an injectable weight loss medication to oral semaglutide (Wegovy).

However, while real-world studies like this can provide useful insights into how treatments work in everyday life, these findings do not prove that switching to the Wegovy pill caused the weight loss.

The results have not yet been published in a peer-reviewed scientific journal.

Wegovy pill linked to 4.1% weight loss after switching

This was a retrospective cohort analysis evaluating weight change, treatment adherence, and patient satisfaction after people switched from injectable semaglutide (Wegovy) or tirzepatide (Zepbound) to oral semaglutide for weight management.

The study included a random sample of 194 adults with overweight or obesity and at least one obesity-related complication, such as hypertension, cardiovascular disease, obstructive sleep apnea, and dyslipidemia.

Participants had no history of diabetes or bariatric surgery and had an average baseline weight of 221.6 pounds (100.5 kg) and an average body mass index (BMI) of 34.5 kg/m2. 

Participants switched from an injectable GLP-1 medication to oral semaglutide between January 5 and February 7, 2026. There was no requirement for how long they had used an injectable medication or how much weight they had lost before switching.

Participants were required to use oral semaglutide regularly for 3 months and self-report their weight within 30 days of starting treatment and again at the end of the study period.

After 3 months of taking oral semaglutide, participants had lost an average of 4.1% of their body weight, or 8.8 pounds.

At the 3-month mark, 2 in 5 participants (40.7%) had lost 5% or more of their body weight.

The proportion of participants with a BMI of 30 or greater also decreased by 21.6 percentage points, from 87.1% at baseline to 65.5% after 3 months.

Most participants (82.4%) reported at least one treatment improvement. Around 69% reported better-fitting clothes, and around 51% reported eating healthier.

Betul Hatipoglu, MD, Medical Director, Diabetes & Metabolic Center at University Hospitals, Cleveland, OH, who wasn’t involved in the study, told Healthline that, “These findings are encouraging because they suggest that some people can continue losing weight after switching to oral treatment.” 

However, she also noted, “I would view this as promising early evidence supporting another treatment option, rather than proof that switching improves results.” 

What to consider when choosing Wegovy pill vs. injection

Mir Ali, MD, medical director of MemorialCare Surgical Weight Loss Center at Orange Coast Medical Center in Fountain Valley, CA, who wasn’t involved in the study, noted that because the oral medication is absorbed through the gastrointestinal tract, less of it reaches the bloodstream compared to the injectable form. 

However, oral semaglutide can still be effective for weight loss.

“While studies show that the injectable form is more efficacious, current findings demonstrate that the oral version is also effective for weight loss,” said Ali. 

Some people may prefer the oral form for ease of use and transportation because it does not require refrigeration. However, it must be taken in the morning on an empty stomach, followed by a 30-minute wait before eating, Ali told Healthline.

“Both formulations can cause gastrointestinal side effects, including nausea, vomiting, diarrhea, and constipation. Taking a pill does not eliminate those risks,” Hatipoglu said.

Hatipoglu also said factors to consider include how well your current treatment is working, side effects, your comfort with injections, your daily routine, cost, and insurance coverage.

She added that the decision should be made with a prescribing clinician, who can help plan the transition and monitor your response.

“Both options can be effective, so patients should discuss the risks and benefits of each with their provider. Ultimately, these medications are tools to support a healthier diet and lifestyle, and patients who make these fundamental changes achieve the best results,” said Ali.

#4760. Toyota Land Cruiser Recall Sees Over 105,000 J250-Gen SUVs Dealing with Possible Fuel Leak, Fire Risk

The automaker has conducted a years-long investigation into the issue, and now they’re looking to bring back the vehicles for a fix where needed.

Toyota has issued a rather strange recall for the 2024-2027 Land Cruiser. It also seems rather comprehensive; the recall population numbers 105,794, which is nearabout the total number of Land Cruisers sold in the U.S. so far for the current J250 generation.

The issue stems from a retaining clip that connects fuel tubes from the gas tank to the engine. Specifically, there’s a resin fuel tube on the tank that connects to a main metal fuel tube, the latter of which delivers gas to the engine. Toyota says that if the retaining clip on the connector between the two is lifted, there’s a possibility of a fuel leak—and of course, a fuel leak means an increased chance of fire.

Toyota conducted a years-long investigation into this issue, starting all the way back in July 2024. You can read the full timeline of events here, but we’ll summarize the six-page document for you.

Toyota became aware of the issue when field reports noted the retainer clip holding the fuel tubes together was lifted or missing. How this happened was vigorously investigated, but Toyota found no issues in the part or its manufacturing processes. One theory suggested in the report is that some recovered connectors showed evidence of markings that could’ve been caused by someone disconnecting the tubes after manufacturing. Additionally, other markings seen on certain clips were consistent with prior supplier testing.

When it comes to the fire risk, Toyota says it evaluated the temperatures of nearby components, finding all of them below the ignition temperature of fuel.

The most recent testing from Toyota this year attempted to identify how the retainer clip at this connection could become lifted. None of the tests provided the information Toyota was looking for; that said, among 400 field vehicles where no connector disconnects had been alleged / leaks reported, Toyota identified one with a lifted retainer clip. Despite the clip being lifted, that one vehicle was not leaking fuel.

Toyota ultimately decided to conduct this recall based on its fuel leak volume evaluation, and the number of field cases it observed of the clip being lifted. As of this recall, Toyota is aware of 23 field reports and 64 warranty claims that relate to the issue. With the cause and circumstances around how the issue manifests, Toyota is unsure what percentage of vehicles might contain this defect.

The fix is relatively simple. Dealers will inspect the connection and install a supplemental clamp. If necessary, they’ll replace the resin fuel tube, too. Notifications to owners are expected to occur by December 13 this year, and per federal law, all recall work will be completed free of charge.

#4759. Ubisoft were working on a second Assassin's Creed game set in Qin Dynasty China, if this allegedly leaked footage is to be believed

Assassin’s Creed Destiny was its working title, report claims

Footage alleged to show a prototype of a cancelled Assassin’s Creed game in action has popped up online. The game, it's claimed, was set in ancient China, went by the working title Assassin’s Creed Destiny, and was a separate project to Chinese-set mobile game Assassin’s Creed Jade. Instead, from the sounds of it Destiny would have been a game aimed at PC and consoles.

MP1st claim to have unearthed this in-development look at AC ThatBungieShooter from developer portfolios and that the game was being headed by Tencent Games subsidiary TiMi Studio Group. Set in the Qin Dynasty period - as the still in the works Jade is - AC Destiny's events would reportedly have taken place in 206 BCE, putting a few years of distance between the two.

The majority of the alleged Destiny footage shows a female assassin fighting her way into a sacred palace that's under siege by real world Chinese warlord Xiang Yu, with possession of a mysterious artefact on the line. Very much an Assassin's Creed game, then. Those hooded roof-jumpers do love their shiny baubles of indeterminate power. There's also some footage of fighting animations playing out in the grey-walled prisons of dev testing environments.

Destiny appears to have gotten the axe following Tencent's decision to scale back from working on big-name Western series in favour of upping their focus on a select pool of big budget games that don't come with chunky royalty fees to other firms.

Assassin's Creed Jade didn't end up getting the axe, but does still remain without a release date as of writing. It was last briefly mentioned by Ubisoft during a March rundown of everything going on Assassin's Creed land from the series' head of content Jean Guesdon.

If you're after an AC game set in China that you can play on PC, 2015's Assassin’s Creed Chronicles: China exists. It's a 2.5D spot of stabbing set during the Ming dynasty and stars Ezio-trained female assassin Shao Jun. It's also where I got the featured image for this article, though I must stress it's not one I've played - it lurks within the lengthy list of ACs I might eventually get to if I get time between now and the end of time.

#4758. Detached Nails More Than 4 Times Higher Among GLP-1 Users, Study Finds

  • GLP-1 drugs like Ozempic have been linked to unusual nail changes in a new study.
  • GLP-1 users reported nail detachment more than four times as often as nonusers, and nail discoloration affected 46% of GLP-1 users, compared with 17% of nonusers.
  • The differences held up among people who had lost weight, suggesting weight loss alone may not explain them.
  • A companion study also notes that hair loss is a common side effect of GLP-1s.
  • Experts share tips for preserving skin and hair health while taking GLP-1 medications.

GLP-1 users have reported various unusual side effects like “Ozempic feet,” “Ozempic butt,” and “Ozempic breasts.” These changes have been largely tied to rapid weight loss.

Now, fingernails have entered the conversation. Nails detaching from the nail bed or changing color may be more common among people taking GLP-1 drugs, according to a new study.

In the study, which researchers presented in October at the European Academy of Dermatology and Venereology (EADV) Congress 2026 in Vienna, people taking GLP-1 drugs such as semaglutide — Ozempic, Wegovy — and tirzepatide — Mounjaro, Zepbound — reported nail detachment and nail discoloration more often than people who didn’t take the drugs.

When the researchers counted only nail changes that started after treatment began, the link between GLP-1 use and nail detachment nearly disappeared, study author Charles Taïeb, MD, PhD, told Healthline.

“This is why we insist that a substantial part of the nail excess reflects the patients’ overall health rather than the drug itself, while a modest drug-specific effect remains plausible,” Taïeb said.

Earlier research has also linked GLP-1 use to hair loss. In the new study, the researchers explained why they examined nails, noting that nails and hair both grow from cells that divide quickly.

However, the researchers cautioned in a press release that the study can’t establish that the drugs caused the nail changes. Both the nail study and a companion hair study captured self-reported symptoms at a single point in time, a design that can identify associations only, they said.

Nail changes reported among GLP-1 users

For the study, researchers examined a representative sample of people in France, the United States, Brazil, and Mexico.

Participants included 575 people taking GLP-1 drugs and 1,329 people with type 2 diabetes, obesity, or both conditions, who had never taken GLP-1 drugs.

The average treatment duration was 18.5 months, and researchers tracked weight loss over three months.

They also looked separately at people who had lost weight, to help tell whether the drugs or the weight loss explained the changes. However, the abstract didn’t specify which GLP-1 drugs participants took.

The researchers have submitted the findings to peer-reviewed dermatology journals, where they are under review, Taïeb told Healthline. Here’s what they found.

Nail separation found in 39% of GLP-1 users

According to the American Academy of Dermatology (AAD), nail detachment, or onycholysis, occurs when a nail lifts, and the lifted area often appears white.

Taïeb told Healthline that the study asked about “nail detachment” because patients, not doctors, filled out the questionnaire.

Nail detachment was the strongest signal among the five nail changes the researchers asked about, Taïeb said.

According to the abstract, 39% of GLP-1 users reported nail separation or detachment (onycholysis), compared with 9% of nonusers.

Nail discoloration, other nail changes found in 17% of GLP-1 users

Nail discoloration is a change in a nail’s usual color, such as white, yellow, or greenish-black, according to the AAD.

According to the abstract, 46% of GLP-1 users reported discoloration, compared with 17% of nonusers. Ridges or grooves and fragile nails were also more common among GLP-1 users.

Mir B. Ali, MD, a bariatric surgeon and medical director of the MemorialCare Surgical Weight Loss Center at Orange Coast Medical Center in Los Angeles, said that while he prescribes GLP-1s, nail detachment hasn’t come up in his practice. Ali wasn’t involved in the research.

“I haven’t had anybody complain of that. It’s not something we really see,” he told Healthline. He said his patients more often mention brittle nails or minor changes in nail color.

Weight loss alone didn’t explain GLP-1 nail changes

Over a three-month period, 76% of GLP-1 users reported weight loss, compared with 47% of nonusers, according to the abstract.

Weight loss alone didn’t appear to explain the nail findings, the researchers said in the press release. The differences between users and nonusers held up when they looked only at people who had lost weight.

The researchers also cautioned in the press release that the study can’t show the drugs caused the changes. They noted that about half of GLP-1 users had nail problems before starting treatment.

Adjusting for skin conditions and nutritional deficiencies weakened some of the associations, they said, though nail detachment and discoloration stayed about two to three times as common among users.

Those adjustments relied on conditions participants reported, including iron, vitamin B12, zinc, and vitamin D deficiencies, as well as psoriasis, eczema, and other skin conditions, Taïeb said.

“GLP-1 medications may affect the nails indirectly rather than directly,” said Tanya Evans, MD, a board certified dermatologist and medical director of the Skin Cancer Program at the Melanoma Clinic at MemorialCare Saddleback Medical Center in Laguna Hills, CA, who wasn’t involved in the research.

Because the drugs reduce appetite, some people may eat less protein, iron, and zinc, which can contribute to brittle nails, discoloration, or nail detachment, Evans told Healthline. Rapid weight loss may also temporarily disrupt nail growth, much as it can trigger hair shedding, she said.

Ali said the total amount of weight a person loses plays a bigger role in hair and nail changes than the rate at which they lose it.

GLP-1 hair loss and scalp changes

The U.S. labels for Wegovy and Zepbound list hair loss as a side effect reported in clinical trials.

The labels for the Ozempic, both injectable and pill form, as well as Mounjaro, list hair loss as a side effect, based on reports received after the drugs entered the market. Those reports don’t establish how often it happens or whether the drugs caused it.

As such, researchers have been examining GLP-1-related hair loss.

A companion study of the same participants found hair and scalp changes were also more common among GLP-1 users, according to that study’s abstract, also provided to Healthline:

  • Unusual hair loss: 50% of users versus 34% of nonusers
  • Loss of hair volume or density: 42% versus 19%
  • Hair loss that started after treatment began: 26% versus 12%

Scalp redness was about three times as common among users, and most users with hair loss also had scalp symptoms such as itching, redness, or scaling.

Scalp inflammation may play a role in hair changes among GLP-1 users, in addition to the shedding that often follows rapid weight loss, one of the study’s authors stated in the news release.

“This raises the possibility that inflammation may be contributing in some patients, rather than the problem being solely the classic temporary hair shedding associated with rapid weight loss,” Evans said.

Experts have told Healthline that hair loss on GLP-1 drugs is often temporary and tied to weight loss.

“Hair loss, we see commonly, not only in patients who are on GLP-1 medications, but also in our surgical weight loss patients,” Ali said.

“When you lose weight, especially in women, it releases hormones. You’re getting metabolic changes, and all these things can lead to hair loss and some changes in the nails.” Ali said he tells patients that “as the weight stabilizes at a healthy weight, the hormones stabilize and the hair tends to come back to where it was before.”

How to protect your hair and nails while using GLP-1s

Nail detachment and discoloration don’t appear as adverse reactions in the current U.S. prescribing information for Ozempic, Wegovy, Mounjaro, or Zepbound.

Ali said the best thing patients can do is get the right nutrition, including enough protein. He said he also advises patients to take a multivitamin along with B-complex, vitamin D, and calcium.

“I would not recommend stopping the GLP-1 medication simply because nail detachment occurs,” Evans said. She advised people to tell their doctor about any new changes in hair, scalp, or nails.

Treatment for nail detachment or discoloration depends on the cause, Evans said. A dermatologist can check for an infection, an inflammatory nail disorder, a nutritional problem, an injury, or another explanation, she said.

A detached nail recovers as a new nail grows out from its base, Evans said. That takes about 4 to 6 months for a fingernail and 9 to 12 months for a toenail.

Lastly, the AAD recommends having a board certified dermatologist examine any nail that’s lifting.

The AAD also recommends seeing a dermatologist if skin, hair, or nail side effects don’t improve, as they can help determine whether symptoms are due to the medication or another condition.

“The important message is don’t panic, but don’t ignore persistent nail changes either,” Evans said.

#4755. Bitcoin miners' move into AI is creating a new market for trading computing power

Bitcoin mining companies are increasingly turning to artificial intelligence (AI), renting out computing power and converting mining facilities into data centers. Now, some of the companies that built their businesses serving bitcoin miners are following them into the AI market.

Luxor Technology, best known for its bitcoin mining services, is also moving into AI hardware and trading contracts that allow companies to agree on computing prices in advance.

The Seattle-based company said on Tuesday that it raised $16 million in a round led by CMT Digital. Bitcoin mining equipment makers Canaan and MicroBT also invested. Luxor said the money will go toward its AI hardware brokerage and computing-power trading business.

The round included new investment and sales of existing shares, although Luxor did not disclose how much of the new funding was invested in the company.

Publicly traded bitcoin miners had signed more than $70 billion worth of AI and high-performance computing contracts by the end of March, according to CoinShares data.

Not everyone is moving away from bitcoin mining. Nasdaq-listed NIP Group (NIPG) announced plans last week to essentially increase its mining capacity by about 48%. The company said it expects to generate about 195 bitcoin a month at full deployment.

Moving into AI also requires more than access to electricity. Ethan Vera, Luxor's chief operating officer, told CoinDesk that its software business works with bitcoin miners representing nearly two gigawatts of power capacity.

After assessing more than half that capacity, Luxor found that most sites had sufficient electricity and fiber connections for AI workloads but would require new data centers.

Luxor is also expanding into contracts that allow buyers and sellers to agree on future computing prices, much as bitcoin miners use financial contracts to manage changes in mining revenue.

Sam Hallene, a partner at CMT Digital, said AI companies have different reasons to protect themselves against price changes, including the possibility that expensive computing equipment remains useful longer than expected.

"We don’t know exactly what the market structure will look like at the end of the day," Hallene told CoinDesk.

Luxor already offers privately negotiated contracts tied to computing rental prices, although the market is still developing. Vera said transparency remains a problem, particularly around AI hardware prices.

In August, Luxor introduced a price index that tracks daily asking prices for Nvidia AI server systems, providing buyers and sellers with a reference point for equipment prices.

#4753. General Motors Is About to Bet Big on a New, Affordable Electric Vehicle Platform

Lithium manganese-rich batteries and a streamlined vehicle development process will define GM’s future EVs.

General Motors laid out its technology blueprint for the coming years on Wednesday, and it seems a new electric vehicle platform is on the horizon.

The Michigan-based manufacturer announced a slew of upcoming technologies at its GM Forward event in New York City on October 21. Artificial intelligence, renewed investment in autonomous driving technology, and new software-defined vehicles were all on the menu, but it was the plans for GM’s fourth-generation electric vehicles that specifically caught our eye. Going forward, General Motors will focus on spreading EV ownership across its whole customer base.

All-new battery chemistry and redesigned drive motors make up the core of GM’s future EV plans. New lithium manganese-rich batteries will underpin GM’s highest-volume EV segments, targeting a balance of range, durability, and affordability. Produced in partnership with LG Energy Solution in Spring Hill, Tennessee, these batteries will be 33 percent more energy-dense than lithium iron phosphate; as such, they will offer longer driving range in a smaller, lighter battery pack at comparable prices to lithium iron phosphate ones. Overall, GM is targeting 20 percent more range from every kilowatt-hour of energy.

“This technology will appear in our highest-volume segments, and it's a great example of how we're bringing battery innovation to mass-market EVs, with lower costs, long range, and improved performance,” GM president Mark Reuss said on Wednesday. “One of every four vehicles sold around the world is an EV. That number is only rising. We're not walking away from that. We're embracing it. We continue to develop our future EVs and work to reduce battery costs. And when the U.S. market is ready for EVs in large numbers, we will be there for them.”

GM says developing a new battery chemistry typically takes years of building and physically testing hundreds or thousands of cell designs. This time, GM engineers used computer modeling to test more than 300,000 battery designs before building a single physical prototype. The time between the first LMR battery pack concept and formal program approval was just one month, thus speeding up GM’s fourth-generation EV platform.

Simplicity will be key to streamlining GM’s future EV models. As a result, a new family of electric motors and power electronics trims the number of parts inside each drive unit by 40 percent; these smaller, lighter e-motors lower production costs and open up space for more people and cargo, GM said. Future GM EVs will also have fewer control modules and 30 percent less wiring than current-generation models. Perception, propulsion, active safety, and chassis controls will be consolidated into a single, high-speed “Central Compute Unit.” The fourth-generation EV platform will feature 40 percent faster processing speeds as well.

An improved production process will also be key to GM’s fourth-generation EVs. Future manufacturing teams can test production steps, review equipment placement, and identify safety or ergonomic concerns before new hardware is installed. A factory intelligence platform, called “PlantBrAIn,” will combine plant-floor data with computer models to improve quality on fourth-gen electric vehicles and battery packs. The renewed process is already working, too; battery packs at GM facilities are currently running through the process 30 percent faster than before.

In addition to cutting production costs, GM is razor-focused on reducing development time. Instead of the current five-year vehicle development timeline, GM wants to develop a car in two years. Of course, artificial intelligence is just the tool to do it: Designers, engineers, and manufacturers will now be connected on a unified digital thread from the very first day of development, and instant communication will allow for a concurrent engineering model that will, supposedly, speed up the development pace.

“We're replacing this entirely sequential process with a new operating system built on large-scale data and concurrent development. What we're building collapses design, engineering, and manufacturing into a set of probabilistic optimizations that create better products in half the time,” Sterling Anderson, chief product officer at GM, said. “In the age of AI, data is currency. Large companies like General Motors have a reputation for being large, for having a lot of inertia, for being slow. In the age of AI, the data and the scale that we have stops being a drag and becomes an advantage.”

Finally, GM is continuing to invest in autonomous drive systems. Last year, the automaker announced autonomous highway driving would arrive by 2028, and pre-production vehicles are now being tested. And it won’t just be GM EV customers who benefit from hands-free highway driving; autonomous highway driving capabilities will soon expand to GM’s most popular gas-powered vehicles. This system will also be powered by the Central Compute Unit.

“As we move towards a more autonomous future, it becomes what you need it to be when you need it-a genuine thinking space, a planning space, a space to recharge. It really extends your life onto the road instead of pausing it until you get to your destination,” Sebastian Bauer, head of human interface design at GM, said. “None of this works without trust, and trust is not a feature. It is a foundational design principle, and one that means adapting to your conditions and your needs, and being very transparent about the decisions that the vehicle is making so that you always understand why.”

After learning to ride at 17, Emmet worked in the motorcycle industry before joining Autoweek in 2022 and Road & Track in 2024. The woes of alternate side parking have kept his fleet moderate, with a 2014 Volkswagen Jetta GLI and a BMW 318i E30 street parked in his Queens community.

#4752. Pain Prevalence Higher in Women, Increases Most Rapidly Before 55

Follow Healthline on our socials for all things health.

Women experience a higher prevalence of pain than men across different areas of the body, according to a large global study published October 5 in Nature Medicine.

The analysis, which included more than 6 million people, also found that pain prevalence generally increased with age, with the most rapid increases occurring before age 55.

The findings come as lower back pain, neck pain, and osteoarthritis — all common causes of pain — are projected to become more prevalent globally by 2050.

Pain, especially lower back pain, is the leading cause of disability worldwide. However, researchers have lacked a standardized way to examine how self-reported pain varies across the life span and different regions of the world.

This new study is one of the largest efforts to date to examine self-reported pain using a standardized approach.

The researchers analyzed data from 6,125,459 individuals spanning 118 countries from 1990 to 2025. Participants ranged in age from 5 to more than 100, and 55% were female.

They used self-reported data on pain across 11 body sites:

The researchers found that women consistently reported a higher prevalence of pain across all 11 sites than men.

The largest differences between women and men were in facial pain, headache, and stomach or abdominal pain.

Pain prevalence also increased most rapidly before age 55, especially at musculoskeletal sites such as the hips, back, and knees.

The researchers found that the prevalence of pain varied considerably by body region and differed by age.

Back pain was the most commonly reported, affecting 40% of participants, while facial pain was the least common at 2%.

Neel Anand, MD, a board certified orthopedic spine surgeon and director of the Cedars-Sinai Spine Center in Los Angeles, who wasn’t involved in the study, explained some factors that may contribute to the high prevalence of back pain.

“Our modern lifestyles involve more prolonged sitting, less physical activity, and more time spent looking down at phones, tablets, and computers, all of which can contribute to poor posture and increased stress on the spine,” he said.

“As we get older, the discs between our vertebrae naturally lose moisture and elasticity, making them more vulnerable to degeneration, bulging, and herniation. Excess weight and smoking can accelerate this process,” Anand added.

In addition to pain at individual body sites, the researchers examined three broader measures: any bodily pain, high intensity pain, and generalized pain.

Any bodily pain was defined as pain reported at any site. High intensity pain was defined as an average pain intensity of 7 or higher on a 0–10 scale. Generalized pain was defined as pain occurring in 4 of 5 body regions according to the 2016 fibromyalgia spatial criteria.

The prevalence of any bodily pain increased progressively across the life span, from around 30% at age 5 to 70% among people ages 100 and older. All three pain measures were consistently higher in women than men.

While any bodily pain increased with age, high intensity and generalized pain peaked in mid-to-late life before declining.

High intensity pain peaked around age 50, while generalized pain peaked around age 70.

These different patterns could help identify when prevention and pain management efforts may be most beneficial across the life span.

Vernon Williams, MD, a sports neurologist and founding director of the Center for Sports Neurology and Pain Medicine at Cedars-Sinai Orthopedics and Sports Medicine in Los Angeles, who wasn’t involved in the study, told Healthline there are several signs that pain should be evaluated by a doctor.

“People shouldn’t feel that they simply have to live with pain. I especially recommend talking with a doctor when pain persists, interferes with sleep or daily activities, limits exercise or function, or is accompanied by symptoms such as numbness, tingling, or weakness,” Williams said.

“Persistent pain deserves an evaluation to identify the underlying cause and develop an appropriate treatment plan.”

The researchers found that smoking, obesity, and lower household income together accounted for 18.3% of the global pain burden across all body sites.

People who currently smoked had a modest but consistently higher prevalence of pain across body sites from adolescence through age 65. The strongest association was seen with facial pain.

Obesity was increasingly associated with pain beginning in early adulthood, particularly in weight-bearing joints such as the knees, hips, feet, and ankles.

Lower household income was also associated with a higher prevalence of pain across all body sites beginning in early adulthood. The strongest association was seen with chest pain.

People with the lowest household incomes were more than twice as likely to report chest pain as those with the highest incomes. This difference was greatest in midlife and narrowed at older ages.

Williams noted that medication isn’t the only option for managing pain.

“Regular, appropriately paced exercise can help change how the brain experiences pain, while mindfulness, breathing exercises, adequate sleep, and other behavioral strategies can help the nervous system better regulate the pain response,” he said. “When medication is needed, I encourage patients to discuss non-opioid and non-pharmacologic options with their doctor as part of a comprehensive approach.”

Anand added that, “The best strategy remains prevention and preservation of function: maintaining a healthy weight, strengthening the muscles that support the joints, and avoiding smoking can all help reduce the strain placed on the musculoskeletal system.”

#4751. 2027 Cadillac Escalade-V Collector Series Celebrates 125 Years of Luxury

The 125th Anniversary Collector Series honors Cadillac’s history by dressing a limited run of Escalade-Vs in special-edition attire.

In the early 1900s, the Wright brothers were experimenting with flight, Henry Ford was developing the Model T, and Albert Einstein was making finishing touches to his theory of relativity. At the same time, Cadillac was emerging with the 1903 Runabout, which made 10 horsepower from a single-cylinder engine and cost $850—about $33,300 in today’s economy.

Next year will mark 125 years since Cadillac’s founding, and a lot has changed since the Runabout’s release. To celebrate the luxury brand’s milestone anniversary, Cadillac is releasing the 2027 Escalade-V 125th Anniversary Collector Series, which will have a very limited production run.

See all results for new 2027 Cadillac Escalade / Escalade ESV for sale near 100009

The Anniversary Collector Series is all about exclusivity. While buyers can’t choose much beyond the exterior paint color in terms of the vehicle’s appearance, they can add a bit of personalization by selecting their own VIN and serial number.

To complement the Midnight Stone Frost or Vibrant White Tricoat paint, the Escalade-V 125th Anniversary wears forged 24-inch wheels and features blue brake calipers, dark-finish badges, puddle lamps with heritage V-8 graphics, and carbon-fiber details on the grille surround, lower rear fascia, mirror caps, and engine cover.

This special edition doesn’t change much under the hood, but the Escalade-V is plenty powerful as-is. A supercharged 6.2-liter V-8 makes 682 horsepower to cart the massive SUV to 60 mph in just 4.3 seconds in our testing.

Inside, Santorini Blue stitching and seatbelts connect Cadillac’s racing history with the special-edition Escalade-V. The blue accents pair with a sleek black-and-gray interior, Collector Series floormats, exclusive interior badging, and a blue key fob.

The Escalade-V 125th Anniversary Collector Series will be limited to 252 units and sold only in the United States and Canada. Orders are open now, with pricing starting at around $239,000.

#4750. Scam alert: The government is not deleting your phone number

Published Oct 22, 2026 4:15 PM EDT

The scams are getting a bit meta. The Federal Trade Commission (FTC) has issued a warning about a new scam currently making the rounds. But this time, the con artists aren’t impersonating a politician or celebrity to trick consumers.They’re claiming that they are the ones sounding the alarm at the FTC.

Real FTC officials explained the general scheme in an alert released on October 22. Scammers call a target to tell them that bad actors in another country have registered a separate phone using their name and number for nefarious purposes. The imposter may even “transfer” you to a fake police officer to file a report. Fake badge numbers and IDs are also offered to make it all sound legitimate, and skeptics may even be directed to verify everything by searching for the police department’s phone number online (also fake). Failure to comply allegedly results in the loss of your personal phone number.

Those who follow through with the instructions provide scammers with their state-issued ID and other personal info for the bogus police report, but it’s not over yet. Some time later, targets will receive another call informing them that they will soon be arrested for those nonexistent crimes committed using their phone number. The solution? Transfer money for a security deposit.

“None of this is true—it’s all a scam,” assured the FTC.

To prevent unnecessary headaches, the FTC listed a few ironclad rules. Actual FTC employees will never contact you about sending money, or transfer you to someone else requesting funds. The FTC will never threaten to disconnect your phone number, or ask you to keep your conversations secret from others. Finally, they will never text a photo of the supposed employee ID as a way to verify their identity to you.

Unfortunately, combatting scammers is a bit like playing a neverending game of Whack-a-Mole. While the FTC has a list of common ways to spot government imposters, they encourage everyone to report the latest con jobs to ReportFraud.ftc.gov.
